CHAPTER: IX

Environmental Awareness, Public Participation and Training

Date: September 16, 2024

GPCB observed World Ozone Day 2024 with impactful
awareness programs across its regional offices,
emphasizing global and national efforts to protect the
ozone layer and mitigate climate change. Activities were
aligned with the UNEP theme “Montreal Protocol:
Advancing Climate Action” and engaged stakeholders
including industries, students, science centres, and local
communities.

Regional Office - Ahmedabad (Rural)

GPCB Ahmedabad (Rural) organized a comprehensive
awareness session at the Conference Room of Laxcon Steel
Industry, Changodar. The event began with a welcome
address by Shri S.S. Desai, Assistant Environmental
Engineer. He emphasized the importance of World Ozone
Day, celebrated annually on 16th September to raise
awareness about the ozone layer and reinforce
international commitment toward its protection.

Shri Desai elaborated on the India Cooling Action Plan
(ICAP), an initiative to reduce cooling demand, improve
energy efficiency, and phase out ozone-depleting
substances (ODS). He highlighted the alignment of ICAP

with India's obligations under the Montreal Protocol and
appreciated the proactive steps taken by the refrigeration
and air conditioning industries to adopt energy-efficient
systems and low-GWP refrigerants.

An open discussion with industry representatives followed,
focusing on technological advancements, challenges in
implementing sustainable cooling solutions, and best
practices in transitioning toward environmentally friendly
alternatives. The session served as a platform for capacity
building and reinforcing stakeholder collaboration in
ozone layer protection.

Regional Office - Bhavnagar

In collaboration with Kalyan Regional Science Centre,
Bhavnagar, the regional office organized a series of
competitions—painting, essay writing, and elocution—at
A.V. School, Bhavnagar. Students participated
enthusiastically, spreading awareness through creativity
and public speaking.

Regional Office - Gandhinagar

To promote World Environment Day 2024 celebration at
Alang Beach Bhavnagar by Regional office Bhavnagar. in
partnership with Nisarg Community Science Centre,
organized a public rally advocating the use of cloth bags.
The event was attended by Mr. B.P. Prajapati (Scientific
Officer) and Mr. Rahul Patel (Assistant Environmental
Engineer). Renowned eco-activist Shri Kantikaka from
Rajkot, accompanied by his wife, inspired attendees by
carrying placards and displaying cloth bags as amessage of
intergenerational environmental responsibility.

The participants took a pledge to protect the ozone layer
and contribute to broader environmental conservation.
Cloth bags were also distributed to residents in vegetable
markets in Sectors 21 and 24 of Gandhinagar.

Regional Office - Himmatnagar

An awareness program was organized at M/s Sabarkantha
District Co-operative Milk Producers Union Ltd. (Sabar
Dairy), Himmatnagar, as part of World Ozone Day and in
accordance with CPCB guidelines for environmental
management of dairy farms and Gaushalas.

Regional Officer Mrs. N.D. Ajmera addressed the gathering
on the importance of ozone layer protection and the
relevance of the 2024 theme. Sabar Dairy representatives
shared their sustainability initiatives, including the
installation of solar plants for member farmers,
decentralized biogas systems, and ration-balancing
programs for cattle welfare.

The event also included a presentation by GPCB officials on
the CPCB's dairy farm guidelines, followed by a Q&A
session. The program concluded with a vote of thanks anda
shared commitment to environmentally responsible dairy
practices.

9.2 Important events and visits to the Board by Experts

(1) Ambaji Padyatra at Head office Gandhinagar:

With the mantra of “Ambaji Padyatra-Swachh Paryavaran
Yatra” associated with the faith of Mai devotees, a special
initiative has been taken by GPCB for the last 13 years to
recycle and dispose of thousands of tons of various solid
waste collected during the Ambaji Padyatra through
scientific methods. As part of this initiative more than 300
tons of waste have been disposed of on the Ambaji Padyatra
route so far. This was stated by Forest and Environment
Minister Shri Mulubhai Bera.

A flag-off ceremony was held today at GPCB Gandhinagar
under the chairmanship of Forest and Environment
Minister Shri Mulubhai Bera and in the presence of Minister
of State Shri Mukeshbhai Patel to flag off a team of about
100 volunteers, 10 Bolero vehicles, 10 e-rickshaws and e-
bikes with the aim of properly disposing of various waste
found onthe Ambaji Padyatra route.

Forest and Environment Minister Shri Mulubhai Bera said
that “this year, as part of a new initiative, Ambaji walkers
will be given one steel bottle instead of five plastic
bottles. For this, 5,000 steel bottles have been donated by
various industry associations. More than 50 street plays
will be performed during the walk to create awareness
about avoiding the use of single-use plastic.”

Fairs and festivals are the symbol of Indian culture. Through
festivals, the new generation is irrigated with rituals. Lakhs
of devotees walk and have darshan of Maa Amba with faith
in the Bhadarvi Poonam fair held at Ambaji for years. To
serve these walkers, service camps have been started by
various associations, villages and cities on the walk route.
Meanwhile, since 2011, the GPCB has been carrying out a
continuous mission to keep the walk route clean with the
help of various organizations to dispose of various wastes
generated naturally. Keeping the walk route clean is the
duty of all of us. Congratulating the voluntary organizations
and youth participating in this campaign, the Minister also
requested the villages and cities coming on the walk route
tovoluntarily join the campaign.

Minister Shri Mulubhai said that the society is also
participating along with the government to complete the
target of Gujarat in the 'Ek Pad Maan Ke Naam' campaign
given by the Prime Minister, which is a matter of pride for us.
On the birthday of Prime Minister Shri Narendrabhai Modi,
on September 17, a 'Swachhta Mahabhiyan' is to be held in
Gujarat under the leadership of Chief Minister Shri

Bhupendrabhai Patel, so we all will have to make special
efforts to increase public participation in this service work,
the Minister added on this occasion.

Minister of State for Forest and Environment Shri
Mukeshbhai Patel said that “the Prime Minister's Swachh
Bharat Mission has become a mass movement in the true
sense today. It is commendable that GPCB and voluntary
organizations are working for the prompt and proper
disposal of the waste collected during the Ambaji
Padyatra. Along with reducing the use of single-use
plastic, the environment will also have to be preserved.
For the first time, a cloth bag ATM has been installed in
Sector-21 Gandhinagar to use cloth bags instead of
plastic bags.”

To realize the Prime Minister's dream of net zero, rainwater
along with solar energy will have to be harvested. The
Minister added that joint efforts will have to be made to
plant more and more trees under the 'Ek Pad Maan Ke
Naam' campaign to make all the cities of the state green like
Green City Gandhinagar.

Speaking on the occasion, Principal Secretary, Forest and
Environment Department, Shri Sanjeev Kumar said that
“Every year, with the help of GPCB and voluntary
organizations, a great work is being done to keep the
Ambaji Padyatra route clean. Disposal and recycling of
solid waste not only saves the environment but also
provides employment.”

Giving the details of the program, the Chairman of GPCB,
Shri R.B. Barad, in his welcome speech, said that “Every
year lakhs of pilgrims come to Ambaji on foot to
participate in this Maha Mela. Food, drink and rest
facilities are provided to the pilgrims through various
service camps. Various types of waste are collected on
these roads, which must be collected and disposed of
scientifically. This year too, the Gujarat Pollution Control
Board has organized “Ambaji Padyatra- Clean
Environment Yatra” in coordination with Gujarat
Dyestuff Management Association (GDMA) and Nepra
Foundation. Cloth bag ATM machines have been installed
in important pilgrimage places of the state to prevent the
use of plastic. In the coming month, it is planned to install
about 250 ATM machines at Amul parlours at different
places to ensure that cloth bags are available."

An initiative has been taken to install reverse vending
machines for plastic bottles at the main ST bus stands and

4. Environment Clinic On “Plastic Waste Recycler
& Producer's EPR Registration” At Regional
Office Gandhinagar:

An Environment Clinic was organized by GPCB, Regional
Office, Gandhinagar, on 18th January 2025. The topic of the
clinic was "An Awareness Program on Plastic Waste
Recycler & Producer's EPR Registration." The primary
objective of this session was to inform and guide
stakeholders about the Plastic Waste Management Rules,
2016, its amendments, and the process for Extended
Producer Responsibility (EPR) registration for producers
and recyclers of plastic waste.

The event was held at the seminar hall of Shilp Gravures,
Kalol, and was attended by various industrialists and
stakeholders actively involved in plastic production,
recycling, and waste management.

The session was inaugurated by Shri Sanjay V. Malsattar,
Regional Officer, GPCB, Gandhinagar. He highlighted the
growing environmental concerns related to plastic waste
and stressed the importance of collective efforts from
producers, recyclers, and the public to address these
challenges. He acknowledged the role of the GPCB in
facilitating awareness programs that encourage
compliance with environmental regulations and help in
achieving sustainable waste management practices.

Presentations and Discussions

The main segment of the session was a detailed
presentation delivered by Shri V R Ghadge and his team,
who provided an in-depth overview of the Plastic Waste
Management Rules, 2016, and its amendments. They also
explained the key aspects of the Extended Producer
Responsibility (EPR) registration process.

+ Plastic Waste Management Rules, 2016 and
Amendments

+ The Plastic Waste Management Rules, 2016 were
established to address the environmental impacts of
plastic waste. These rules mandate that producers,
importers, and brand owners manage the plastic
waste generated by their products.

CHAPTER: IX

Environmental Awareness, Public Participation and Training

+ Amendments to the rules have made the EPR
framework more comprehensive and applicable to a
wider range of plastic products. These amendments
include:

+ The addition of more types of plastic packaging
underthe EPR system.

+ A stronger emphasis on recycling and waste
collection targets for producers.

+ The implementation of a monitoring and
compliance system for tracking the progress of
waste management activities.

+ EPRRegistration Process

The session provided a step-by-step explanation of the EPR
registration process, which is crucial for producers to
legally manage the plastic waste generated from their
products. The following aspects of EPR registration were
discussed:

+ — Required Documents:

+ Proof of company registration (e.g., business
license, GST registration).

+ Details of plastic waste generated and managed by
thecompany.

+ Plans for collection, recycling, and disposal of
plastic waste.

* Compliance with applicable environmental
standards.

+ — Registration Method: The producers and recyclers are
required to register with the relevant regulatory
authority (such as GPCB or CPCB), submit their waste
management plans, and meet specific collection and
recycling targets.

Impacted Stakeholders

The program emphasized the role and responsibilities of
various stakeholders affected by the Plastic Waste
Management Rules, 2016, and EPR registration:

+ Producers and Brand Owners: Producers are
responsible for the collection and recycling of plastic
waste generated by their products. They must register
for EPRand implement waste management practices.

+ — Recyclers: Companies involved in the collection and
recycling of plastic waste play a critical role in ensuring
that plastic waste is processed in an environmentally
responsible manner.

* Consumers: While not directly involved in the
registration process, consumers are integral to the

success of plastic waste management by adhering to
proper disposal and recycling practices.

+ Regulatory Authorities: GPCB and CPCB are
responsible for monitoring compliance with the rules
and ensuring that producers and recyclers meet their
obligations under the EPR framework.

Feedback and Conclusion:

The session was well-received by the industrialists and
stakeholders in attendance. The participants appreciated
the clarity provided on the legal requirements for plastic
waste management and the EPR registration process.
Several attendees expressed interest in further
collaborating with GPCB to ensure that their operations
align with environmental regulations and sustainability
goals.

In conclusion, the Environment Clinic successfully
highlighted the importance of compliance with the Plastic
Waste Management Rules, 2016, and provided practical
guidance on EPR registration. The event contributed to
raising awareness about plastic waste management and
the significant role played by producers, recyclers, and
regulatory bodies in ensuring a cleaner and greener
environment.

6 Mission LiFE at GPCB

Mission LiFE was introduced by Prime Minister Narendra
Modi—at COP26 in Glasgow on 1 November 2021—as a
mass movement for “mindful and deliberate utilization,
instead of mindless and destructive consumption “to
protect and preserve the environment.”

It aims to nudge individuals and communities to practice a
lifestyle that is synchronous with nature and does not harm
it. Those who practice such a lifestyle are recognized as Pro
Planet People India is the first country to include Life in its
Nationally Determined Contributions: “India will put
forward and propagate a healthy and sustainable way of
living based on its traditions and the values of conservation
and moderation, including through a mass movement for
LIFE, asa key to combating climate change.”

Objectives of Mission LiFE:

It is designed with the objective to mobilise at least one
billion Indians and other global citizens to take individual
and collective action for protecting and conserving the
environment in the period 2022-28.Within India. Mission
LiFE will be carried out by making individual
environmentally friendly behaviour a mainstream
movement. Mission LiFE is led by NITI Aayog and
implemented by the Union Ministry of Environment,
Forests, and Climate Change

Activities carried out under Mission LiFE program by GPCB:

Over 200 events have been conducted by GPCB under
Mission Life, like Clean up drives, nature walk, plantation
drives, plastic collection drives, poster competition crafts
and art Workshops, for the awareness and public outreach
across the Gujarat. Over the year GPCB made efforts to
spread awareness about mission life's goals through
broadcasting Radio jingles for 46 days on All the Radio
channels across the state, 4 Street play campaign including
200 show of play Ambaji padayatra for proper disposal of
plastic waste, Eco friendly Ganesha Chaturthi celebration
by awareness for idol immersion as per guideline of CPCB,
Celebration of Diwali by reducing the use of fire crackers to
reduce air and noise pollution and Mission life.

+  GPCB as part of the celebration of “Mission Life” also
put up a stall at the Sahakar Bharati Mandali
Adhiveshan-2024 displayed Banners promoting the
adoption of lifestyle as per the action points/objectives
of “Mission Life” and ban on single use plastic was
displayed, an estimated 40,000 people visited the stall
of GPCB. GPCB Bhavnagar successfully conducted this
public awareness program.

+  GPCB also supports “All India Cycling Tour called as
Ride to Free Air” for promotion of Mission LiFE within
Gujarat in collaboration with Mizoram Pollution
Control Board.

Various Activities aligned with Mission Life themes
have been Carried out by Regional Offices of GPCB

general administration and accounts etc.

CHAPTER: X
Prosecution launched and offences

Prosecution launched and disposal cases for breach of Environmental laws

In its early years, the Board focused on persuading industries to comply with the provisions of the Water Act, 1974. However,
despite issuing notices and allowing sufficient time to implement pollution control measures, many industries failed to
comply. Consequently, the Board had to initiate legal action under the Water Act, 1974, the Air Act, 1981, and the Environment
(Protection) Act, 1986 against industries that did not adhere to the provisions or directions issued under these Acts.

The Board has issued notices of direction, closure orders, and filed prosecutions against defaulting units. The actions taken
under the Water Act, 1974, the Air Act, 1981, the Criminal Procedure Code (CrPC), and the Environment (Protection) Act, 1986,
as of 31/03/2025, areas follows:

Any other important matters dealt by GPCB

13.1 Publication of Environmental
Guidelines for Ready-Mix Concrete
(RMC) Plant

The government of Gujarat has prioritized infrastructure
development to support economic growth and attract
investment. Rapid urbanization along with
industrialization has recently seen a surge in demand for
Ready Mix Concrete Plants (RMC Plants).

Ready Mix concrete plants are often located near
construction sites in urban areas for logistical reasons. Due
to this proximity, there may be a greater probability that
residential properties will be close to the plant, putting
inhabitants at risk of exposure to fine air pollutants and
noise.

In Gujarat, there was no any guideline available which
covers Environment Management System (EMS) aspects of
the RMC plant. To streamline and standardize the technical
and Environment Management System (EMS) aspects of
RMC plant, there is a need felt to frame a specific guideline
which covers all technical points regarding EMS.

To prepare this guideline, the Board has formed a
committee of many experts, such as Professor of L.D.
College of Engineering, Regional Director of CPCB, Deputy
Executive Director of Cll-IGBC, Chairman of RMC Plant
Association and representative of RMC Plant Manufacturer.
This environmental guidelines for RMC Plant released on
12.07.24.

Strict adherence to the said guideline will lead to a
significant reduction in air pollution and noise pollution
caused by RMC Plant and will have a positive impact on the
health of the citizens.

Environmental Guidelines for
Ready-Mix Concrete (RMC) Plant

13.2 Organization for Economic Co-
operation and Development (OECD)-
GPCB Workshop on BAT with
International Experts

GPCB, in collaboration with the Organisation for Economic
Co-operation and Development (OECD) Secretariat and the
Expert Group on Best Available Techniques (BAT),
successfully organized a two-day international workshop
on November 26-27, 2024. The workshop, titled "Capacity
Building on BAT," aimed to enhance understanding of BAT
principles in the textile and paper & pulp sectors, with a
focus on improving environmental protection and sectoral
implementation. The event featured expert presentations
from environmental agencies of Germany, Korea, the
United Kingdom, and the United States, facilitating rich
cross-country knowledge exchange on BAT. Over a hundred
delegates participated, including representatives from
OECD, UBA, GIZ, CPCB, IRO-MoEFCC, various SPCBs, GCPC,
Officials of GPCB, concerned industry associations, NGOs,
and international experts. During the sessions GPCB's
pioneering launch of India's first BREF document for the
textile sector and the initiation of a similar initiative for the
paper recycling industry was highlighted. A technical site
visit to Arvind Mills, Khatraj, Gandhinagar offered OECD
officials valuable insights into the real-time application of
sustainable production practices and innovative
technologies adopted in the textile industry. The workshop
provided a dynamic platform for international
collaboration.

13.3 Development of BREF for the
Wastepaper Recycling Sector

Following the successful completion of the BREF (Best
Available Techniques Reference) document for the textile
sector, efforts have advanced toward the preparation of a
BREF for the wastepaper recycling sector within the paper
industry. As a significant milestone, a strategic partnership
Memorandum of Understanding (MoU) was signed last year
between the Gujarat Paper Mill Association and GPCB, with
external support from UBA and GIZ.

During the reporting year, the following key
activities were undertaken

- A kickoff meeting of the Technical Working Group
(TWG) in presence and guidance of UBA officials and
GIZ was held on June 11, 2024, to initiate the BREF
development process. The meeting focused on
defining the purpose, scope, and structure of the
document, identifying key environmental issues and
techniques to be considered, and designing the
industry questionnaire.

13.4 Two Days' Training Program on "ESG -
Business Responsibility and
Sustainability Report (BRSR) Training -
The Practitioner's Perspective"

GPCB, in collaboration with GSFC University, Vadodara,
organized a two-day training program on April 19th and
20th, 2024. The theme of the program was "ESG - Business
Responsibility and Sustainability Report (BRSR) Training -
The Practitioner's Perspective."

The training saw participation from around 35 industries
across the State, reflecting strong interest in integrating
Environmental, Social, and Governance (ESG) practices
into business operations.

Shri Niraj Shah, Environmental Engineer, delivered a
lecture highlighting BRSR as a new, though not mandatory,
approach from a practitioner's perspective. The
importance of ESG and BRSR in promoting sustainable
industrial growth and corporate transparency was
emphasized, with a focus on regulatory expectations, data
reporting, and aligning business practices with
sustainability goals.

The training provided a platform for industries to learn
about the new concept of BRSR and understand
approaches for its effective implementation in their
operations.

13.5 Initiative by the board for better
Implementation of E-waste
(Management) Rules, 2022

To strengthen local-level monitoring and enforcement
under the E-Waste (Management) Rules, 2022, Board has
communicated to Urban Development and Housing
Departments, as well as to 17 Mahanagar Palikas, outlining
the responsibilities of Urban Local Bodies (ULBs) under this
rule. Furthermore, recognizing the emerging need for
managing solar photovoltaic waste, GPCB has appointed
the Council on Energy, Environment and Water (CEEW) to
carry out a comprehensive study on solar waste generation
and management across Gujarat. This initiative will help
assess existing infrastructure, identify gaps, and support
the development of a future-ready policy framework for
solarwaste management.

13.6 Drone Survey of Rivers:

CPCB classifies river stretches into Priority | to Priority V
based on maximum BOD values. Priority | indicates the
most polluted river stretch, droping to Priority V, which
indicates the least polluted.

The board does not permit industries or units to directly

GUJARAT POLLUTION

CONTROL BOARD
GANDHINAGAR

discharge industrial wastewater into rivers. However, in
some circumstances, due to geographical factors,
industrial and domestic wastewater may be discharged
without treatment. To investigate unauthorized discharges
in polluted river stretches, modern techniques are used for
surveys. Previously, the board completed drone surveys of
the Sabarmati river and Amlakhadi. Subsequently, drone
surveys of five other polluted river stretches—Bhadar,
Bhogavo, Khari, Vishwamitri, and Dhadhar—were
conducted to identify illegal discharges in their stretches,
especially outfalls that discharge untreated wastewater.

13.7 Details of Qualifying Tests for
Environmental Auditors (Ongoing)

Environmental Audit Scheme has been implemented by
GPCB since 1997 following the Hon. Gujarat High Court's
order dated 20/12/1996. The scheme involves selecting
experts from various institutions, including government,
semi-government, educational institutions, R&D centers,
and private companies/consultants working in
environmental management and pollution control to act as
environmental auditors under Schedule-1 and Schedule-2.
The Environmental Audit Committee reviews the
capabilities of team members and inspects their
laboratories. Based on this review, the committee
recommends to the GPCB whether to recognize the
environment auditors as Schedule-1 or Schedule-2.

To strengthen the environmental audit scheme, the board
has decided to conduct online qualification tests for
applicants. Qualified candidates will be included as team
members in Schedule-1 and Schedule-2 environment
audits, and further actions regarding their applications will
be taken accordingly.

13.8 Detailed Audit/Study of Pesticides
Industry (Ongoing)

Environmental Audit Scheme has been implemented by
GPCB since 1997 following the Hon. Gujarat High Court's
order dated 20/12/1996. Under this scheme, the relevant
allotted industries are audited annually by recognized
environmental auditors. CPCB classified 17 types of
industries as "Highly Polluted Industries." For the year
2024-2025, the board plans to conduct detailed audits and
studies of these 17 types of industries, including Pesticides
(technical) (excluding formulation), at an estimated cost of
10 crore rupees. Accordingly, 49 pesticide (technical)
industries in the state have been audited by 38 Schedule-1
environmental auditors.

13.9 National Clean Air Programme (NCAP)

The MoEF&CC has launched National Clean Air Program
(NCAP) in January 2019 as a time bound strategy to tackle
air pollution at both Regional and Urban scales.

NCAP envisages reduction by 20-30% in PM10
concentration over baseline in year 2017 by 2024. Target
has been revised to achieve reduction in PM10 level up to
40% or achievement of national standards (60 4g/m3) by
2025-26.

CPCB declared 130 cities of India in the list of 'Non-
attainment Cities' under National Clean Air Programme
(NCAP) which do not meet the National Ambient Air Quality
Standards prescribed under the Air Act 1981. Three cities of
Gujarat namely Ahmedabad, Surat and Vadodara are Non-
Attainment Cities and Rajkot come under Million-plus City
as per list of 'Non-attainment Cities’: Anmedabad, Surat,
Vadodara and Rajkot cities have received total fund of
%1193.91 crore under XVFC for implementation of NCAP of
which total 7998.32 crore had been utilized. The Cities
Action Plans for all four cities have been approved by CPCB
and uploaded on 'PRANA Portal’ and are under

implementation. Detail related to air action plan for the
Non-attainment Cities of the State is on PRANA Portal which
is available on web link: https://prana.cpcb.gov.in. Source
Apportionment Study for Ahmedabad and Surat have been
completed while Vadodara and Rajkot are completed and
draft report are under process for finalization.

Three Tier Committee has also been constituted to ensure
effective implementation of NCAP viz. Steering Committee -
Headed by Hon'ble Chief Secretary, Monitoring
Committee- Headed by Hon'ble ACS/PS, F&E Department,
and Implementation Committee-Headed by Hon'ble
Municipal Commissioners of concerned Cities.

Gujarat State Action Plan (GSAP) is under the National
Clean Air Programme (NCAP) is approved by the Steering
Committee headed by Hon'ble Chief Secretary,
Government of Gujarat and is under implementation.

Surat has secured the first prize of 21.50 Cr. in the 'Swachh
Vayu Sarvekshan' for 2023-24 conducted by

MoEF &CC.

13.10 Environment Audit Scheme

For the improvement of environment management system
to achieve norms of pollution parameters, environment
laws and environment challenges and for the sustainable
development of the State, 'Environment Audit Scheme’
was introduced by the Hon'ble Gujarat High Court vide
order dated 20/12/1996, 13/03/1997, 16/09/1999, and
22/04/2010 in the state. Hon'ble Gujarat High Court has
empowered GPCB for implementation this scheme in
Gujarat.

During the course of time, it is felt that many industries,
clusters and estates are benefited by the Environment
Audit Scheme, as qualified technical professionals became
a link between industries on the one hand and GPCB and
other public authorities as well as association of the
industries on the other hand with the added vital elements
of accountability and transparency.

Environment Audit Committee comprises of environment

experts from different institutions and chaired by the
Member Secretary, GPCB. After receiving the application for
the environment auditor, the Members of this Environment
Audit Committee review the auditor's team members,
inspect the laboratory and accordingly recommend GPCB
for recognition as Schedule-| and Schedule-II Environment
Auditors.

Auditors shall apply for the Schedule-I and Schedule-II
auditor with team comprises of minimum 4 members:

1. Environmental Engineer
2. Chemical Engineer

3. Microbiologist

4. Chemist.

Each auditor can audit maximum 15 industries per audit
team throughout the year. Schedule - | auditors are consist
of government colleges/ reputed institutes/ R & D centers
etc of the State. Schedule - Il auditors are generally private
firms/consultants working in the field of EMS and pollution
control.

GPCB has come out with a publication "ENVIRONMENT
AUDIT SCHEME At a Glance". This publication gives an
overallidea about the scheme, its implementation process,
benefits and various other aspects related thereof.

Currently 53 Schedule-| and 134 Schedule-Il Environment
Auditors are recognized by the Board, under Environment
Audit Scheme.

13.11 Scheme forthe educational field

GPCB, as a regulatory authority with extensive experience,
believes that the solution to certain types of pollution
problems is too complex which cannot be solved
immediately. Addressing these challenges requires long-
term, in-depth research and the identification of detailed,
practical solutions. To effectively tackle such
environmental concerns and enhance pollution control
measures, the Board encourages the involvement of
candidates engaged in Research and Development (R&D)
and pursuing Ph.D. programs.

The primary objective of this initiative is to harness the
benefits of research for the Board, society, sustainable
development, and the overall welfare of the State. Students
engaged in environmental studies can gain valuable
knowledge, guidance, and orientation on various
environmental issues i.e. technical, scientific, legal, and
more through interaction with experienced Board officers
and domain experts. These candidates may also utilize the
Board's infrastructure to support their research
endeavours.

To promote and support this effort, the GPCB has launched
a dedicated scheme offering financial assistance for Ph.D.
candidates focused on pollution control. This scheme was
approved during the 190th Board Meeting and officially
published on 04/03/2014, with subsequent amendments
made on 23/04/2015. Under this initiative, a “Research
Advisory Committee” comprising distinguished experts in
the field of education and environment has been
constituted.

In addition, the GPCB has institutionalized an internship
program aimed at students from engineering and science
disciplines, further fostering academic collaboration in the
field of environmental protection.

Research and Development through Ph.D. Scheme:

+ Selected candidate under this scheme will be provided
financial assistance of Rs. 20,000/- (Twenty thousand)
permonth.

+ Pertinent expenses inclusive of expenses like typing,
stationery, photography, training visit, participating in
conferences within or outside the State for Ph.D./
Research exertion. The Member Secretary has been
delegated powers to sanction expenditure not
exceeding Rs. 60,000/- (Sixty thousand) per year (per
candidate).

* Candidate can use the laboratory and library of GPCB
for their research work.

Internship Scheme:

GPCB in collaboration with Gujarat Cleaner Production
Centre (GCPC), implemented a internship scheme to
provide graduate and postgraduate students with
hands-on exposure to environmental issues and
practical solutions. GPCB has launched an online
portal to invite applications from students for
internship. Students have been selected based on their
academic qualification and area of interest for
internship. These students represent leading
institutions such as L.D. College of Engineering, MS
University Baroda, and others.

The internship starts with a structured 7-day
orientation program covering topics like wastewater
treatment, air pollution control, cleaner production
and EIA etc. Interns then assign to regional offices,
CETPs, TSDFs, and industries for project work. Their
progress is monitored through weekly reports and
attendance records.

The program concludes with a final viva and
presentation of project work.

Total 40 students have completed internship training in
year 2024-25 in GPCB.

GPCB will also launch a summer internship program
from next year to accommodate more interns.

13.12 Vehicle Location Tracking System
(VLTS)

To ensure effective control over the transportation of
hazardous waste across the state, the Gujarat Pollution
Control Board (GPCB), in collaboration with NIC
Gandhinagar and NIC Uttarakhand, has implemented the
Vehicle Location Tracking System (VLTS). This system
enables real-time, GPS-based monitoring of hazardous
waste movement from the waste generator to the final
disposal or reuse facility.

In accordance with the Hazardous and Other Wastes
(Management and Transboundary Movement) Rules, 2016,
hazardous waste is required to be transported with a
manifest to ensure environmentally sound disposal. The
waste is disposed of only at GPCB-authorized facilities such
as landfills (TSDFs), incinerators, co-processing units, or
recycling units.

Under VLTS:

+  AlS-140 compliant GPS devices are installed in all
vehicles transporting hazardous waste.

+ Around 15,000 vehicles have been registered on
the VLTS portal.

. On average, 1,300-1,400 trips are monitored daily.

+ Transporters must pre-define routes; deviations
trigger alerts to both GPCB and the concerned
industry.

+ Advanced features like geo-mapping, geo-
fencing, and hotspot mapping are integrated into
thesystem.

The VLTS is also integrated with the Xtended Green Node
(XGN) Portal, minimizing the need for manual
documentation and improving digital governance.

Vehicle Trip Route

= sSurenaranagar

Ps

GUJARAT POLLUTION

CONTROL BOARD
GANDHINAGAR

GPCB

Additionally, the e-Manifest system has been introduced,
enabling transparent and efficient tracking of hazardous
waste consignments.

The VLTS has significantly strengthened hazardous waste
inventory management, enhanced compliance, and
established a systematic, self-regulatory mechanism for
industries. Beyond ensuring environmental and safety
compliance, VLTS also facilitates the reuse and recycling of
hazardous waste, promoting sustainable industrial
practices.

13.13 IEC activities carried out by Board
under E-waste (Management) Rules,
2022

\In line with the E-Waste (Management) Rules, 2022, the
Gujarat Pollution Control Board (GPCB) in collaboration
with the Gujarat Environment Management Institute
(GEMI), organized a capacity-building and awareness
programs across the state. Twelve programs were
conducted in districts such as Gandhinagar, Anmedabad
(Rural), Bharuch, Bhavnagar, Mehsana, Palanpur,
Himmatnagar, Anand, Surat, and Vapi, with participation
from over 690 stakeholders. These programs focused on
equipping E-waste producers, recyclers, manufacturers,
and bulk consumers with practical knowledge on
compliance, extended producer responsibility (EPR), safe
recycling methods, and the legal framework under the E-
waste (Management) Rules, 2022. Moreover, six collection
drives have been carried out in collaboration with
stakeholders and NGOs across the state.

13.14 International E-waste Day Celebration

A technical seminar was organized on 14th October 2024,
being International E-Waste Day, A total of 65 participants
attended the seminar, including key stakeholders. The
seminar featured four detailed sessions covering the
regulatory framework, circular economy, E-waste
management technologies, and solar panel recycling. An
interactive Q&A session was held to address queries and
encourage active discussion.

13.15 Yoga Day Celebration

An invaluable gift of ancient Indian tradition, Yoga has
emerged as one of the most trusted means to boost
physical and mental well-being. The word "Yoga" is derived
from the Sanskrit root yuj meaning "to join", "to yoke" or

"to unite", symbolizing the unity of mind and body; thought
and action; restraint and fulfilment; harmony between
human and nature, and a holistic approach to health and
well-being.

This year marks the 10th International Day of Yoga with the
theme “Yoga for Self and Society.” Yoga, a transformative
practice, represents the harmony of mind and body, the
balance between thought and action, and the unity of
restraint and fulfillment. Itintegrates the body, mind, spirit,
and soul, offering a holistic approach to health and well-
being that brings peace to our hectic lives. Its power to
transform is what we celebrate on this special day.

Yoga Day was celebrated by the GPCB, an initiative of the
Government of India, on June 21, 2024, at the head office,
Gandhinagar. The celebration started with the speech of
our Hon'ble Chairman Shri R. B. Barad. He encouraged
officers to practice regular yoga to remain fit and improve
concentration. The Hon'ble member secretory Shri D.M.
Thakar addressed the participants on the importance of
yoga practice in our life in which yoga guru Mrs. Deepaben
explained the benefits of yoga to all the staff and made
them perform various yoga poses. In celebration of Yoga
Day, around 250 employees of GPCB Head office
participated enthusiastically. After warm up exercises, all
the staff members practiced & performed sitting and
standing asanas, importance of these were explained
simultaneously. All the staff members were taught the
importance of Yoga in their life & how to maintain the
harmony between body mind and soul. International Yoga
Day celebrations ended with a huge success

13.16 Circular Economy Initiatives in Gujarat

In alignment with the Government of India's national vision
for transitioning towards a Circular Economy, the State of
Gujarat has proactively initiated multiple strategies to
promote resource efficiency, reuse, recycling, and recovery
while minimizing environmental impacts.

Key initiatives undertaken include:

+ Extended Producer Responsibility (EPR)
Implementation: Gujarat Pollution Control Board
(GPCB) has strengthened enforcement of EPR
obligations for various waste streams such as e-waste,
plastic waste, used oil, battery waste, and waste tyres.
Dedicated monitoring and reporting systems are being
institutionalized for effective compliance.

+ Development of Common Environmental
Infrastructure: The state promotes common effluent
treatment plants (CETPs), hazardous waste treatment-
disposal sites, co-processing units in cement plants,
and waste-to-energy projects, recycler and actual
users in waste management, facilitating
environmentally sound waste management.

+ Waste Collection Drives and Public Engagement:
Regular campaigns are organized for plastic and e-
waste collection, beach clean-ups, and citizen
awareness to encourage source segregation, reduce
littering, and channel waste into formal recycling
systems.

+ Capacity Building & Skill Development: Special focus is
given to capacity building of regulatory institutions,
training of ULB officials, and skill development
programs for entrepreneurs, recyclers, and informal
workers to enhance local circular economy
ecosystems.

+ GPCB in collaboration with the Gujarat Cleaner
Production Centre (GCPC), is developing a
comprehensive action plan for CE transition. The two-
stage stakeholder consultation engaging industries,
recyclers, informal sector actors, and technical experts
across waste streams like e-waste, plastic, battery,
tyres, hazardous waste, and used oil has been
conducted in year 2024.

+ KeyFocus Areas of Draft Action Plan:

+ — Resource efficiency and cleaner production in
industries

+ Reuse and high-value recycling of priority waste
streams

+ Strengthening environmental infrastructure &
governance mechanisms

+  Greenjob creation and skillenhancement

+  Public-private partnerships for innovation and
technology adoption

These actions position Gujarat as a front-runner in
adopting CE principles, building sustainable systems, and
creating an enabling ecosystem for climate-resilient, low-
carbon, and inclusive economic growth..
